| RK-6.3-002 | structural | must | untested: That a transport channel implements deliver() is its class's shape, which no step reads: every scenario's transports are delivered to through it, which the delivery units of Section 10.2 cite. Python's tests/test_channels/test_sms.py::test_deliver_with_provider delivers through a built-in transport; roomkit-rs's conformance runner builds its scripted transports on TransportChannel (crates/roomkit/tests/conformance/scripted.rs). | | | |
| RK-6.3-003 | structural | must | decided gap: step, RMK-1955: Needs one of the implementation's own transport channels or providers in the runners, which build scripted channels only; roomkit-rs has none (tier 1b), feature name transport_provider proposed. | | | |

| RK-6.3-005 | structural | must | decided gap: step, RMK-1955: Needs one of the implementation's own transport channels or providers in the runners, which build scripted channels only; roomkit-rs has none (tier 1b), feature name transport_provider proposed. | RK-6.3-006 | structural | must | decided gap: writable, RMK-1955: The bindings expectation after video.open and conference.open shows the Video and Conference rows; the two audio-video rows, AUDIO, VIDEO and TEXT, need an audio-video channel fixture (requires video and conference). | | | |
| RK-6.3-007 | structural | must | decided gap: step, RMK-1955: Needs one of the implementation's own transport channels or providers in the runners, which build scripted channels only; roomkit-rs has none (tier 1b), feature name transport_provider proposed. | | | |

| RK-6.4-484 | behavior | must | untested: No implementation offers an experimental ACP protocol version: Python initializes with its stable version 1 and refuses an SDK of another version (channels/_acp_client.py), and roomkit-rs sends its constant version 1 (channel/connector.rs), so no runner can opt into one; the stable version as the default is RK-6.4-483. | | | |
| RK-6.4-485 | behavior | must | `acp/an-agent-that-selects-another-protocol-version-fails-the-turn` | pinned |  |  |
| RK-6.4-486 | behavior | must | untested: No runner launches a process: the corpus serves its scripted agent through the channel's transport seam, with no process (README, « Scripted ACP agents »), so no scenario can observe how a command is started. Each implementation launches the configured command as an argument vector, with no shell: Python's channel refuses a command that is not a sequence, tests/test_channels/test_acp.py::TestACPChannel::test_requires_argument_vector_and_absolute_cwd, and its transport hands the program and its arguments apart to the process it spawns (src/roomkit/channels/acp_transport.py); roomkit-rs crates/roomkit-acp/src/process.rs starts the program with its arguments, without a shell, crates/roomkit-acp/tests/acp/channel.rs::a_message_reaches_the_spawned_agent_and_its_answer_the_room. | | | |
| RK-6.4-487 | behavior | must | untested: No implementation ships an ACP transport other than stdio: Python's ACPTransport and roomkit-rs's AcpTransport are extension points an integrator implements, and the runners' scripted agent stands behind one, so no runner can observe a standardized alternate transport. | | | |
| RK-6.4-488 | behavior | must | `executors/an-acp-session-is-the-executor-s-that-opened-it-and-the-room-s-turns-follow-it` | pinned |  |  |
| RK-6.4-489 | behavior | must | untested: No implementation offers persistent ACP session resumption: a reconnect never resumes a session (Python channels/_acp_client.py, roomkit-rs channel/connector.rs), so no runner can observe a resumed session identifier; its scenario comes with the capability. | | | |

## §6.5 Source Providers

| Unit | Kind | Level | Scenario | Lock | Python | Rust |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RK-6.5-001 | structural | must | decided gap: step, RMK-1955: Needs a scripted source provider in both runners; roomkit-rs has no sources (tier 1b), feature name source_provider proposed. | | | |
| RK-6.5-003 | structural | must | untested: SourceStatus's values are constants of each implementation's enumeration, which no step reads as a list: `source_health` reads the one status a source's health reports (README, « Calls »), and the table of example sources is informative. Python defines it in src/roomkit/sources/base.py and pins its five values, tests/test_sources.py::TestSourceStatus::test_status_values; roomkit-rs has no sources. | | | |
| RK-6.5-004 | behavior | must | untested: That a source calls emit for each inbound message binds a source implementation, which only its own connection shows; the runners' scripted sources stand in for one (README, « Setup », `sources`). The core's side is the emit callback the kit hands a source it attaches, which takes each message through the inbound pipeline: Python tests/test_sources.py::TestSourceMessageEmission::test_emitted_messages_reach_process_inbound. A source proves its own connection in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance; roomkit-rs has no sources. | | | |
| RK-6.5-005 | behavior | must | decided gap: step, RMK-1955: Needs a scripted source provider in both runners; roomkit-rs has no sources (tier 1b), feature name source_provider proposed. | | | |

The core's side in Python is the one reading every provider hands its SDK's failure to (provider_error, src/roomkit/providers/ai/base.py), whose flag a status, a body or the failure's form decides alike, tests/test_providers/test_provider_error.py::test_a_status_decides_whether_a_failure_is_retried, ::test_a_status_written_in_the_error_s_body_is_its_status and ::test_without_a_status_the_failure_s_form_decides, and the loop, which reads the flag alone, tests/test_channels/test_ai_retry.py::TestRetry::test_retries_on_retryable_error and ::TestRetry::test_non_retryable_error_raises_immediately. A provider proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.7-100 | behavior | must | untested: Which status a failed generation carries is an obligation on a provider implementation, which only its vendor's wire shows; the corpus never drives a vendor's provider (README, « A scenario »). The core's side in Python is the one reading every provider shares: the status the failure carries, else the one its body describes, tests/test_providers/test_provider_error.py::test_a_status_decides_whether_a_failure_is_retried and ::test_a_status_written_in_the_error_s_body_is_its_status. Handing it what its vendor's SDK raised is the provider's. A provider proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.7-101 | behavior | must | untested: That a status of 408, 409, 429 or any 5xx is retryable and any other final is an obligation on a provider implementation, which only its vendor's wire shows; the corpus never drives a vendor's provider (README, « A scenario »). The core's side in Python is the one reading every provider shares, tests/test_providers/test_provider_error.py::test_a_status_decides_whether_a_failure_is_retried, and the loop, which retries on the flag and stops on a final one, tests/test_channels/test_ai_retry.py::TestRetry::test_retries_on_retryable_error and ::TestRetry::test_non_retryable_error_raises_immediately. A provider proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.7-102 | behavior | must | untested: How a failure without a status reads, a lost connection retryable and an answer that does not read final, is an obligation on a provider implementation, which only its vendor's wire shows; the corpus never drives a vendor's provider (README, « A scenario »). The core's side in Python is the one reading every provider shares, tests/test_providers/test_provider_error.py::test_without_a_status_the_failure_s_form_decides, and the loop, which retries on the flag and stops on a final one, tests/test_channels/test_ai_retry.py::TestRetry::test_retries_on_retryable_error and ::TestRetry::test_non_retryable_error_raises_immediately. A provider proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.7-103 | behavior | must | untested: That a 200 whose body is an error object reads as the status it names is an obligation on a provider implementation, which only its vendor's wire shows; the corpus never drives a vendor's provider (README, « A scenario »). The core's side in Python is the reading every provider hands such a body to (error_body_failure), tests/test_providers/test_provider_error.py::test_a_200_whose_body_is_an_error_object_reads_as_the_status_it_names. A provider proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.7-104 | behavior | must | untested: When a failure without a status is retryable from its message is an obligation on a provider implementation, which only its vendor's wire shows; the corpus never drives a vendor's provider (README, « A scenario »). The core's side in Python is the one reading every provider shares, which retries such a failure only when its message names a retried status, a rate limit or an overload, as whole words, tests/test_providers/test_provider_error.py::test_without_a_status_the_failure_s_form_decides. Whether its vendor's SDK dropped a status is the provider's. A provider proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |

Python defines them beside its other provider contracts (src/roomkit/providers/whatsapp/base.py, messenger/base.py, teams/base.py, rcs/base.py, http/base.py, and the STT and TTS provider interfaces of src/roomkit/voice); an implementation of one of those interfaces proves its mapping of its vendor's wire in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance; roomkit-rs has no transport providers (tier 1b). | | | |

## §6.8 Classifiers

| Unit | Kind | Level | Scenario | Lock | Python | Rust |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RK-6.8-002 | behavior | may | — | | | |
| RK-6.8-003 | structural | must | untested: The Classifier interface and its questions are each implementation's API, which no step reads: a classifier is a provider, and no step asks one its questions. Each implementation defines them (Python src/roomkit/classifiers/base.py; roomkit-rs crates/roomkit-ai/src/classifiers/mod.rs and questions.rs) and refuses a choice of fewer than two options and a score of fewer than two levels: Python tests/test_classifiers.py::test_a_choice_needs_two_options_and_a_score_two_levels; roomkit-rs crates/roomkit-ai/tests/classifiers.rs::a_choice_needs_two_options_and_a_score_two_levels. | | | |
| RK-6.8-004 | structural | must | untested: The answers' shapes are each implementation's API, which no step reads: a classifier is a provider, and no step asks one its questions. Each implementation pins them: Python tests/test_classifiers.py::test_answers_read_typed and ::test_check_answers_wants_every_question_with_its_kind; roomkit-rs crates/roomkit-ai/tests/classifiers.rs::answers_read_typed and ::check_answers_wants_every_question_with_its_kind_and_keeps_extras. | | | |
| RK-6.8-005 | behavior | must | untested: That a classifier answers every question or raises ClassifierError, its wait bounded, binds a classifier implementation, which only its service shows; no step asks a classifier its questions. Python's LLMClassifier, on any AI provider, keeps it: tests/test_classifiers.py::test_llm_turns_a_failed_call_into_a_classifier_error (a refused call, and a wait past its bound); roomkit-rs has no such classifier. The core's side is ClassifierError, which the classifier speak policy reads as a failed classification: Python tests/test_speak_classifier.py::test_on_the_channel_a_failing_classifier_lets_the_agent_speak; roomkit-rs crates/roomkit-ai/tests/speaking_admission/turns.rs::the_channel_obeys_real_classifier_silence_and_falls_back_on_its_error. A classifier proves its service's answers in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.8-006 | behavior | must | untested: That a classifier sends the questions' wording as the caller wrote it binds a classifier implementation, which only its service shows; no step asks a classifier its questions. Python's LLMClassifier, on any AI provider, keeps it: tests/test_classifiers.py::test_llm_asks_under_a_schema_and_reads_the_answers and ::test_llm_describes_a_yes_and_a_no_when_given; roomkit-rs has no such classifier. The core's side is the interface it implements, whose questions carry their wording as the caller wrote it (RK-6.8-003). A classifier proves what it sends its service in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.8-007 | behavior | must | untested: How far a classifier's probabilities can be trusted is its implementation's to say, which only its service shows; no step asks a classifier its questions. Python's LLMClassifier, on any AI provider, gives the answer it chose probability 1 and its module's docstring says so (src/roomkit/classifiers/llm.py), tests/test_classifiers.py::test_llm_asks_under_a_schema_and_reads_the_answers; roomkit-rs has no such classifier. The core's side is the interface it implements, whose answers carry probabilities (RK-6.8-004). A trained classifier proves its calibration in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.8-008 | behavior | must | untested: That close() releases what a classifier opened and leaves a handed provider open binds a classifier implementation; no step asks a classifier its questions. Python's LLMClassifier keeps it: tests/test_classifiers.py::test_llm_close_leaves_the_provider_to_its_owner; roomkit-rs has no such classifier. The core's side is that the classifier speak policy never closes the classifier it was handed: Python src/roomkit/speaking/classifier.py (ClassifierSpeakPolicy, whose classifier is closed by whoever made it); roomkit-rs's ClassifierSpeakPolicy keeps the default close of crates/roomkit-ai/src/speaking/policy.rs, by review. | | | |
| RK-6.8-009 | behavior | must | untested: That a classifier whose service reads text alone sends a structured state as its JSON binds a classifier implementation, which only its service shows; no step asks a classifier its questions. Python's LLMClassifier, on any AI provider, keeps it: tests/test_classifiers.py::test_llm_asks_under_a_schema_and_reads_the_answers, the structured state in its prompt as its JSON; roomkit-rs has no such classifier. The core's side is the interface it implements, which takes a state as a text or as JSON (RK-6.8-003). A classifier proves what it sends its service in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.8-010 | behavior | must | untested: That a question the service declines fails the call with ClassifierError binds a classifier implementation, which only its service shows; no step asks a classifier its questions. Python's LLMClassifier, on any AI provider, keeps it: tests/test_classifiers.py::test_llm_turns_a_failed_call_into_a_classifier_error, a refusal raised as ClassifierError; roomkit-rs has no such classifier. The core's side is ClassifierError, which the classifier speak policy reads as a failed classification: Python tests/test_speak_classifier.py::test_on_the_channel_a_failing_classifier_lets_the_agent_speak; roomkit-rs crates/roomkit-ai/tests/speaking_admission/turns.rs::the_channel_obeys_real_classifier_silence_and_falls_back_on_its_error. A classifier proves how it reads its service's refusal in its own tests, outside roomkit's conformance. | | | |
| RK-6.8-011 | non_testable | must | — | | | |
